```json
{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "63126f33-186a-488a-8881-1ce37b7b3594",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "7859f92d-e7b3-488c-836e-003ae3690a11",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "cb446229-b0da-46b4-87c9-fa304e77b15e",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
    "oldString": "confirming that additional content introduces noise.",
    "newString": "indicating that additional content introduces noise.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{A descriptive two-pathway framework.}\nThe results suggest a descriptive two-pathway framework (not a causal model) for why complete traces degrade SFT learning.\n\n\\paragraph{Explicit pathway: diagnostic tokens.}\nThe diagnostic token ratio $d$ (\\S2.1) correlates with accuracy at $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$) across five conditions, and all four models achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% under \\texttt{exception\\_only} with zero variance on Qwen ($\\sigma{=}$0.00, 3 seeds).\nAlthough 55.5\\% of failures are silent logic errors, \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 100\\% fail recall because test assertions generate \\texttt{AssertionError} events.\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}, a strict superset, slightly underperforms \\texttt{exception\\_only} (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that additional content introduces noise.\n\n\\paragraph{Implicit pathway: terminal execution state.}\nTail-truncated traces achieve 93--97\\% accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}) despite $d {\\approx} 0$.\nTail truncation at ${\\geq}$512 tokens captures \\texttt{[Line~X]} variable values at final execution steps, which indirectly encode program outcome; below 384 tokens, the globals module dump consumes the entire budget, yielding ${\\sim}$73\\% ($\\approx$ \\texttt{no\\_trace}).\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic signal dilution in complete traces.}\nBoth pathways' signals exist in full traces but are buried under thousands of intermediate tokens.\nControlled gradient masking confirms this: mean $-$0.61pp under 99.8\\% mask (3 seeds) limits gradient dilution to ${\\leq}$4\\% of the gap (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics} confirms: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps (std\\,=\\,0 by step 200), while \\texttt{full\\_trace} oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence (final std\\,=\\,0.014); eval accuracy CV differs 9.4$\\times$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}\n\\caption{Training dynamics: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges to 99.83\\% by step 150; \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence.}\n\\label{fig:training_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Why SFT over regex?}\nSFT \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%) exceeds the best regex (B5, 99.33\\%) by 0.50pp on caught-exception boundary cases (McNemar $p{=}.014$), provides calibrated probabilities (ECE\\,=\\,0.0027; \\S\\ref{sec:calibration}), and retains 97.65\\% under marker removal vs.\\ B5's 28.49\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\nBidirectional cross-dataset transfer (99.4--100\\%) with near-majority \\texttt{no\\_trace} performance confirms the learned patterns are language-level, not dataset-specific.\n\n\\paragraph{The marginal value of complete traces.}\nSource code alone achieves 73--76\\% (\\texttt{no\\_trace}), so traces add at most ${\\sim}$27pp; complete traces capture only 4--9pp of this headroom while exception events capture 24--27pp.\nThat more information degrades performance aligns with feature selection theory \\citep{guyon2003introduction}, the Information Bottleneck \\citep{tishby2000information}, and LIMA \\citep{zhou2023lima}.\n\n\\paragraph{Design implications.}\nThe two-tier hierarchy suggests a practical fallback: extract exception events first; resort to tail-truncated state otherwise.\nTier~2 benefit is model-dependent (Qwen +10.9pp; CodeLlama +0.9pp), so trace design should match deployment model capacity.\n\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{A descriptive two-pathway framework.}\nThe results suggest a descriptive two-pathway framework (not a causal model) for why complete traces degrade SFT learning.\n\n\\paragraph{Explicit pathway: diagnostic tokens.}\nThe diagnostic token ratio $d$ (\\S2.1) correlates with accuracy at $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$) across five conditions, and all four models achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% under \\texttt{exception\\_only} with zero variance on Qwen ($\\sigma{=}$0.00, 3 seeds).\nAlthough 55.5\\% of failures are silent logic errors, \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 100\\% fail recall because test assertions generate \\texttt{AssertionError} events.\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}, a strict superset, slightly underperforms \\texttt{exception\\_only} (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), indicating that additional content introduces noise.\n\n\\paragraph{Implicit pathway: terminal execution state.}\nTail-truncated traces achieve 93--97\\% accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}) despite $d {\\approx} 0$.\nTail truncation at ${\\geq}$512 tokens captures \\texttt{[Line~X]} variable values at final execution steps, which indirectly encode program outcome; below 384 tokens, the globals module dump consumes the entire budget, yielding ${\\sim}$73\\% ($\\approx$ \\texttt{no\\_trace}).\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic signal dilution in complete traces.}\nBoth pathways' signals exist in full traces but are buried under thousands of intermediate tokens.\nControlled gradient masking supports this: mean $-$0.61pp under 99.8\\% mask (3 seeds) limits gradient dilution to ${\\leq}$4\\% of the gap (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics} confirms: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps (std\\,=\\,0 by step 200), while \\texttt{full\\_trace} oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence (final std\\,=\\,0.014); eval accuracy CV differs 9.4$\\times$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}\n\\caption{Training dynamics: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges to 99.83\\% by step 150; \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence.}\n\\label{fig:training_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Why SFT over regex?}\nSFT \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%) exceeds the best regex (B5, 99.33\\%) by 0.50pp on caught-exception boundary cases (McNemar $p{=}.014$), provides calibrated probabilities (ECE\\,=\\,0.0027; \\S\\ref{sec:calibration}), and retains 97.65\\% under marker removal vs.\\ B5's 28.49\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\nBidirectional cross-dataset transfer (99.4--100\\%) with near-majority \\texttt{no\\_trace} performance confirms the learned patterns are language-level, not dataset-specific.\n\n\\paragraph{The marginal value of complete traces.}\nSource code alone achieves 73--76\\% (\\texttt{no\\_trace}), so traces add at most ${\\sim}$27pp; complete traces capture only 4--9pp of this headroom while exception events capture 24--27pp.\nThat more information degrades performance aligns with feature selection theory \\citep{guyon2003introduction}, the Information Bottleneck \\citep{tishby2000information}, and LIMA \\citep{zhou2023lima}.\n\n\\paragraph{Design implications.}\nThe two-tier hierarchy suggests a practical fallback: extract exception events first; resort to tail-truncated state otherwise.\nTier~2 benefit is model-dependent (Qwen +10.9pp; CodeLlama +0.9pp), so trace design should match deployment model capacity.\n\n",
```

```json
{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "7506cd29-018e-4ca3-9c46-917c43a6ed38",

    "newString": "correlates with accuracy at Pearson $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$, $N{=}5$, df=3; treat as descriptive), but captures only",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\n\\subsection{Task Definition and Diagnostic Signal Metrics}\n\nWe study \\emph{verdict prediction}: given a Python function, a test input, and an optional execution trace, predict whether the test passes or fails.\nThe model produces a single token (\\texttt{pass} or \\texttt{fail}), making this a binary classification task.\nUnlike code generation \\citep{chen2025tracepile} or output prediction \\citep{gu2024cruxeval}, verdict prediction isolates the model's ability to detect faults from its ability to simulate execution, providing cleaner ablation contrasts than generation tasks.\n\nTo quantify signal quality without circular reference to model accuracy, we define the \\emph{diagnostic token ratio} $d = n_{\\text{diag}} / n_{\\text{total}}$, where $n_{\\text{diag}}$ counts tokens matching exception or assertion patterns.\nThis model-free metric correlates with accuracy at Pearson $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$, $N{=}5$ main conditions), but captures only the \\emph{explicit} diagnostic pathway; tail-truncated conditions achieve 93--97\\% with $d {\\approx} 0$, indicating a second, implicit signal channel operating through terminal execution state (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\n\n\\subsection{Dataset}\n\\label{sec:dataset}\n\nWe sampled 10 candidate solutions per problem from Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct (temperature 0.8, top\\_p 0.95) for 591 problems from HumanEval \\citep{chen2021evaluating} (164) and MBPP-sanitized \\citep{austin2021program} (427), executed each against canonical tests with \\texttt{sys.settrace} to obtain line-level traces and pass/fail verdicts, yielding 5,910 pairs (67\\% pass, 33\\% fail).\nThe dataset is split 80/20 by problem ID into 4,720 training and 1,190 test samples (majority baseline: 71.51\\%).\nThe \\texttt{exception\\_only} format collapses passing solutions into identical rows (39.45\\% train, 48.99\\% test duplicates); these duplicates arise from multiple candidate solutions per problem, with the train--test split enforced at the problem level (472 train / 119 test problems, zero crossover; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dedup_stats}).\n\n\\subsection{Trace Representations}\n\nWe define seven conditions that vary the trace information available (Figure~\\ref{fig:trace_overview}):\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\texttt{no\\_trace}: Source code and test input only.\n\\item \\texttt{full\\_trace}: Complete line-by-line trace with variable states.\n\\item \\texttt{VOTA} (Verdict-Optimized Trace Abstraction): Exception events + assertion-point comparisons + loop compression (Appendix~\\ref{sec:vota_algorithm}).\n\\item \\texttt{exception\\_only}: Only exception/error events.\n\\item \\texttt{ae\\_only}: Only assertion-point actual/expected pairs.\n\\item \\texttt{loop\\_only}: Only loop compression summaries.\n\\item \\texttt{VOTA\\_no\\_ae}: VOTA without assertion-point information.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig1_trace_overview}\n\\caption{Overview of the verdict prediction pipeline. Source code and a test input are executed to produce a full raw trace. Four conditions vary the trace information: \\texttt{no\\_trace} (75.13\\%), \\texttt{full\\_trace} (81.44\\%), VOTA (99.05\\%), and \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%). Accuracies for Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$); multi-seed means where available (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}). Appendix~\\ref{sec:vota_examples} provides input/output examples.}\n\\label{fig:trace_overview}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Models and Training}\n\nWe evaluate four models spanning three architecture families: \\textbf{Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B/1.5B-Instruct} \\citep{qwen2024coder}, \\textbf{CodeLlama-13B-Instruct} \\citep{roziere2024code}, and \\textbf{DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct} \\citep{deepseek2024coder}, covering a 1.5B--13B parameter range (8.7$\\times$).\nAll conditions use LoRA \\citep{hu2022lora} (rank=16, $\\alpha$=32, dropout=0.05) on all linear layers, 3 epochs, effective batch size 32, LR $2 \\times 10^{-4}$ with linear warmup/decay, max sequence length 2{,}048 tokens.\nSequences exceeding this limit are truncated while preserving the verdict label.\nThe primary seed is 42; seeds 123 and 456 test \\texttt{exception\\_only} robustness (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_details}).\n\n\\subsection{Loss Ablation}\n\nTo probe the contribution of gradient dilution, we introduce \\texttt{full\\_trace\\_label\\_only}: identical full-trace input but loss computed only on the verdict token (trace positions masked).\nTRL's SFTTrainer computes mean cross-entropy over label tokens, so label-only loss does not amplify the effective learning rate.\nHowever, this ablation simultaneously changes three variables: (1)~which tokens receive gradient updates, (2)~the implicit regularization from predicting trace tokens, and (3)~the training signal distribution.\nThe resulting improvement over standard \\texttt{full\\_trace} provides a confounded upper bound on gradient dilution's contribution.\nA cleaner test (masking 99.8\\% of gradient updates during \\texttt{exception\\_only} training) matches the diagnostic token ratio of full traces while preserving compact input, isolating gradient dilution from content quality (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{A descriptive two-pathway framework.}\nThe results suggest a descriptive two-pathway framework (not a causal model) for why complete traces degrade SFT learning.\n\n\\paragraph{Explicit pathway: diagnostic tokens.}\nThe diagnostic token ratio $d$ (\\S2.1) correlates with accuracy at $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$) across five conditions, and all four models achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% under \\texttt{exception\\_only} with zero variance on Qwen ($\\sigma{=}$0.00, 3 seeds).\nAlthough 55.5\\% of failures are silent logic errors, \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 100\\% fail recall because test assertions generate \\texttt{AssertionError} events.\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}, a strict superset, slightly underperforms \\texttt{exception\\_only} (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), indicating that additional content introduces noise.\n\n\\paragraph{Implicit pathway: terminal execution state.}\nTail-truncated traces achieve 93--97\\% accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}) despite $d {\\approx} 0$.\nTail truncation at ${\\geq}$512 tokens captures \\texttt{[Line~X]} variable values at final execution steps, which indirectly encode program outcome; below 384 tokens, the globals module dump consumes the entire budget, yielding ${\\sim}$73\\% ($\\approx$ \\texttt{no\\_trace}).\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic signal dilution in complete traces.}\nBoth pathways' signals exist in full traces but are buried under thousands of intermediate tokens.\nControlled gradient masking supports this: mean $-$0.61pp under 99.8\\% mask (3 seeds) limits gradient dilution to ${\\leq}$4\\% of the gap (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics} shows: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps (std\\,=\\,0 by step 200), while \\texttt{full\\_trace} oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence (final std\\,=\\,0.014); eval accuracy CV differs 9.4$\\times$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}\n\\caption{Training dynamics: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges to 99.83\\% by step 150; \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence.}\n\\label{fig:training_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Why SFT over regex?}\nSFT \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%) exceeds the best regex (B5, 99.33\\%) by 0.50pp on caught-exception boundary cases (McNemar $p{=}.014$), provides calibrated probabilities (ECE\\,=\\,0.0027; \\S\\ref{sec:calibration}), and retains 97.65\\% under marker removal vs.\\ B5's 28.49\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\nBidirectional cross-dataset transfer (99.4--100\\%) with near-majority \\texttt{no\\_trace} performance shows the learned patterns are language-level, not dataset-specific.\n\n\\paragraph{The marginal value of complete traces.}\nSource code alone achieves 73--76\\% (\\texttt{no\\_trace}), so traces add at most ${\\sim}$27pp; complete traces capture only 4--9pp of this headroom while exception events capture 24--27pp.\nThat more information degrades performance aligns with feature selection theory \\citep{guyon2003introduction}, the Information Bottleneck \\citep{tishby2000information}, and LIMA \\citep{zhou2023lima}.\n\n\\paragraph{Design implications.}\nThe two-tier hierarchy suggests a practical fallback: extract exception events first; resort to tail-truncated state otherwise.\nTier~2 benefit is model-dependent (Qwen +10.9pp; CodeLlama +0.9pp), so trace design should match deployment model capacity.\n\n",
```

```json
    "newString": "Fifth, the gradient masking ablation (\\S\\ref{sec:density}) uses a single model (Qwen-7B, 3 seeds); the complementary label-only analysis is limited to the Qwen family and does not reach significance under clustered bootstrap.\nSixth, VOTA's longer sequences are vulnerable to tokenizer-driven truncation: 1 of 12 model-seed combinations suffered catastrophic failure (DeepSeek seed 42: 84.45\\%), indicating that VOTA robustness depends on tokenizer efficiency.\nSeventh, several baselines (\\texttt{no\\_trace} for 2/4 models, \\texttt{full\\_trace} for 2/4 models) rely on single-seed results; their variance estimates are derived from Wilson CIs rather than empirical seed distributions.",
    "originalFile": "\\documentclass[11pt,a4paper]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n% \\usepackage{acl}  % uncomment for camera-ready (remove [review])\n\\usepackage{times,latexsym}\n\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\graphicspath{{../../figures/paper/}}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{xcolor}\n\\usepackage{algorithm}\n\\usepackage{algorithmic}\n\n\\title{Complete Execution Traces Degrade SFT-Based Verdict Prediction: \\\\A Diagnostic Signal Analysis}\n\\author{Anonymous ACL submission}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nComplete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction (classifying whether a test passes or fails), yet they actively impair SFT learning.\nAcross four code-specialized models (1.5B--13B, three architecture families), complete traces achieve only 77--82\\% while compact exception events reach ${\\geq}$99.33\\%.\nA regex exploiting format markers matches this ceiling, but marker removal collapses it to 28.49\\% while SFT retains 97.65\\%; cross-dataset transfer shows SFT acquires diagnostic patterns beyond format matching.\nGradient masking attributes ${\\leq}$4\\% of the 18pp degradation gap to gradient dilution, suggesting that diagnostic signal density at inference is the dominant factor, though forward-pass attention during masked training is an unquantified residual.\nAn empirical two-tier signal hierarchy holds across families, yielding a practical strategy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{introduction}\n\\input{method}\n\\input{experiments}\n\\input{discussion}\n\\input{related_work}\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur study has several limitations.\nFirst, all experiments use HumanEval and MBPP-sanitized (591 problems, simple assert-based tests on short Python functions); generalization to human-written code, other languages, non-assert test frameworks (e.g., parametric tests, integration tests, flaky tests), and multi-file projects is unverified.\nSecond, all four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; models at 30B+ parameters may have sufficient capacity to overcome diagnostic signal dilution, and general-purpose LLMs may behave differently.\nThird, multi-seed experiments use only 3 seeds (the minimum for variance estimation); larger seed pools would strengthen robustness claims.\nFourth, \\texttt{full\\_trace} was not evaluated on DebugBench due to the supplementary nature of that analysis; whether complete traces degrade on human-written buggy code remains untested.\nFifth, the gradient masking ablation (\\S\\ref{sec:density}) uses a single model (Qwen-7B, 3 seeds); the complementary label-only analysis is limited to the Qwen family and does not reach significance under clustered bootstrap.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work uses publicly available benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized, DebugBench) and does not involve human subjects, crowdsourced annotations, or personally identifiable information.\nAll models evaluated are open-source and publicly accessible.\nTraining uses parameter-efficient LoRA fine-tuning on enterprise-grade GPUs, with each experiment using a single GPU.\nTotal compute is approximately 320 GPU-hours across all conditions, models, seeds, and iterative refinements (including approximately 170 hours for successful experiments; individual runs range from 0.5h for small models to 2.2h for CodeLlama-13B, with the total reflecting 4 models $\\times$ multiple conditions $\\times$ multiple seeds $\\times$ iterative development).\nWe do not foresee direct negative societal impacts from this work; verdict prediction is a developer-facing tool for automated testing.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",
```

```text
695	The high duplication in \texttt{exception\_only} does not affect our experimental conclusions: after deduplication the training set still contains 2,858 unique samples and the test set 607, providing sufficient coverage for the 591-problem benchmark.
696	
697	\paragraph{Problem-level partitioning and sensitivity analysis.}
698	Data is partitioned at the problem level: 472 training problems and 119 test problems, with zero crossover.
699	Duplicates arise from multiple candidate solutions per problem (up to 10 solutions each), not from cross-problem overlap.
700	To verify that deduplication does not alter any finding, we ran a sensitivity analysis across all 19 experimental conditions (7 trace formats $\times$ 4 models, minus conditions not run): deduplication produces a 0.00pp accuracy delta in every case.
701	This confirms that duplicate samples neither inflate nor deflate accuracy, because duplicates share the same ground-truth label and the model's prediction is deterministic at inference time.
702	The \texttt{exception\_only} test set contains 48.99\% duplicate inputs due to representation compactness; all confidence intervals treat samples as independent Bernoulli trials.
703	
704	\begin{table}[t]
705	\centering
706	\caption{Deduplication statistics. The \texttt{exception\_only} format collapses multiple passing solutions into identical rows because correct executions produce no exception events. Train--test input overlap is 0 (no leakage).}
707	\label{tab:dedup}
708	\small
709	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
```

_source line 8750 · 2026-05-22T18:27:26.433Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01LB154zz5aBx91PVhSGhHNQ`</summary>

```text
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/related_work.tex:20:The Information Bottleneck \citep{tishby2000information}, feature selection theory \citep{guyon2003introduction}, and LIMA \citep{zhou2023lima} predict that compressing task-irrelevant information improves learning; data pruning \citep{paul2021deep,sorscher2022beyond} and curriculum learning \citep{bengio2009curriculum} confirm this empirically, consistent with our finding that compact exceptions converge rapidly while verbose traces oscillate.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/experiments.tex:130:Regex baselines confirm this decomposition: B5 (\texttt{[No exception data]}$\to$pass, 99.33\% with markers) collapses to 28.49\% without markers, showing it is entirely format-dependent.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/experiments.tex:195:Beyond 512 tokens, accuracy converges further: multi-seed validation across all 12 model$\times$seed combinations confirms all tail-512 accuracies fall within a 4.63pp band [93.61\%, 98.24\%] (Table~\ref{tab:tail512_seeds}); at tail-1024, three models reach 96.5--98.2\%, compressing the 6.3pp cross-model spread observed at tail-256.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/experiments.tex:197:The residual 2--5pp gap confirms that semantic extraction (identifying exception type and message) still outperforms positional extraction (retaining a fixed window containing non-diagnostic lines).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/experiments.tex:205:\texttt{exception\_only}'s 2 errors have zero overlap with all other conditions' error sets (Jaccard = 0.000; Appendix~\ref{sec:error_overlap}); per-type breakdown confirms all standard exception types achieve 100\% accuracy, with only \texttt{GeneratorExit} (a benign \texttt{BaseException} subclass) causing failures (Appendix~\ref{sec:exception_type}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/appendix.tex:147:In the pass case, the absence of an exception event combined with a matching assertion point confirms correct execution.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/appendix.tex:173:No exception is raised, and the assertion point confirms that the actual output matches the expected value.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/appendix.tex:257:Re-evaluation of the seed 42 checkpoint with the standard max\_length=2048 yields 16\% accuracy, confirming complete model collapse rather than marginal degradation.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/appendix.tex:265:Per-class analysis (Table~\ref{tab:per_class}) confirms that seed 42 achieves 100\% fail precision but only 45.43\% fail recall, systematically defaulting to \texttt{pass}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/appendix.tex:326:This confirms that the model has learned a clean decision boundary: any true \texttt{Exception} subclass in the trace implies failure.
```

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user (Director) is coordinating EMNLP 2026 paper revisions (DDL 2026-05-25) through a Paper Agent role. This session handled seven Director instructions:
   - **De-AI em-dash fixes**: Complete 4 remaining em-dash replacements from prior session
   - **Polish supplement**: Add practical takeaway to Conclusion, confirm subcomponent appendix, verify ≤8 pages
   - **Gradient masking 2-seed update**: Update single-seed numbers to 2-seed across 10 locations
   - **Dangling cross-reference fix**: Investigate `table.caption.3` warning
   - **Gradient masking 3-seed final update**: Update 2-seed to 3-seed across 7 locations
   - **nomarker cross-dataset integration**: Add one result sentence to §3.3
   - **Round 16 审稿修复 (CURRENT)**: Massive multi-priority patch addressing reviewer feedback across entire paper — overclaiming "confirm" replacement, forward-pass hedging, contribution reframe, Abstract simplification, CI transparency, multiple P2 fixes

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - EMNLP 2026 ARR: 8-page main body limit, unlimited references/appendix/limitations
   - Paper Agent communicates with Director only via `paper_agent_post_to_director` (NOT `main_agent_post_to_director`)
   - Evidence strength calibration for verb choice: "demonstrates/shows" (strong, multi-seed+multi-model), "indicates/shows" (medium), "suggests/is consistent with" (weak/confounded)
   - Forward-pass confound: gradient masking only affects backward pass; model still attends to all tokens during forward pass, making the ≤4%/≥96% decomposition an upper/lower bound rather than exact attribution
   - Clustered bootstrap with n_eff=119 (problem-level), Holm-Bonferroni correction
   - Diagnostic token ratio d: Pearson r=0.949 but only N=5, df=3 — descriptive only

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **main.tex** (Abstract + Limitations + Ethics)
     - Abstract rewritten (P0 SYSTEMIC + MF-3 + SF-1 + SF-2 + SF-6):
     ```latex
     \begin{abstract}
     Complete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction (classifying whether a test passes or fails), yet they actively impair SFT learning.
     Across four code-specialized models (1.5B--13B, three architecture families), complete traces achieve only 77--82\% while compact exception events reach ${\geq}$99.33\%.
     A regex exploiting format markers matches this ceiling, but marker removal collapses it to 28.49\% while SFT retains 97.65\%; cross-dataset transfer shows SFT acquires diagnostic patterns beyond format matching.
     Gradient masking attributes ${\leq}$4\% of the 18pp degradation gap to gradient dilution, suggesting that diagnostic signal density at inference is the dominant factor, though forward-pass attention during masked training is an unquantified residual.
     An empirical two-tier signal hierarchy holds across families, yielding a practical strategy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.
     \end{abstract}
     ```
     - Limitations updated with SF-4 (VOTA truncation) and SF-9 (single-seed reliability):
     ```latex
     Fifth, the gradient masking ablation (\S\ref{sec:density}) uses a single model (Qwen-7B, 3 seeds); the complementary label-only analysis is limited to the Qwen family and does not reach significance under clustered bootstrap.
     Sixth, VOTA's longer sequences are vulnerable to tokenizer-driven truncation: 1 of 12 model-seed combinations suffered catastrophic failure (DeepSeek seed 42: 84.45\%), indicating that VOTA robustness depends on tokenizer efficiency.
     Seventh, several baselines (\texttt{no\_trace} for 2/4 models, \texttt{full\_trace} for 2/4 models) rely on single-seed results; their variance estimates are derived from Wilson CIs rather than empirical seed distributions.
     ```

   - **conclusion.tex** (P0 SYSTEMIC + MF-3 + SF-1 + SF-6):
     ```latex
     \section{Conclusion}
     
     Across four code-specialized models (1.5B--13B, three families), complete traces (77--82\%) barely improve over source code alone (${\sim}$73--76\%) while compact exception events achieve ${\geq}$99.33\%; cross-dataset transfer (99.4--100\%) and marker-removal robustness show SFT acquires genuine diagnostic patterns.
     An empirical two-tier signal hierarchy and controlled masking ablation (gradient dilution accounts for ${\leq}$4\% of the gap; content quality at inference explains the majority, though forward-pass information flow is a residual confound) suggest that diagnostic density, not information quantity, is the binding constraint.
     For practitioners building trace-based SFT pipelines, the recommendation is direct: prioritize exception events; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces at ${\geq}$512 tokens provide a viable fallback at 93--97\% accuracy.
     Whether these patterns extend to larger (30B+) or general-purpose models remains open.
     ```

   - **introduction.tex** (P0 SYSTEMIC + P1 MF-4 + MF-5):

     ```latex
     A controlled gradient masking ablation attributes ${\leq}$4\% of the degradation to gradient dilution; content quality (diagnostic signal density at inference) accounts for the majority, though forward-pass attention is an unquantified residual (\S\ref{sec:density}).
     Training dynamics support this: \texttt{exception\_only} converges within 50 steps, while \texttt{full\_trace} oscillates between 82--89\% without stable convergence (\S\ref{sec:ablation}, Appendix~\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).
     ```

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/main.tex",
        "content": "\\documentclass[11pt,a4paper]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n% \\usepackage{acl}  % uncomment for camera-ready (remove [review])\n\\usepackage{times,latexsym}\n\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\graphicspath{{../../figures/paper/}}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{xcolor}\n\\usepackage{algorithm}\n\\usepackage{algorithmic}\n\n\\title{Complete Execution Traces Degrade SFT-Based Verdict Prediction: \\\\A Diagnostic Signal Analysis}\n\\author{Anonymous ACL submission}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nComplete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction (classifying whether a test passes or fails), yet they actively impair SFT learning.\nAcross four code-specialized models (1.5B--13B, three architecture families), complete traces achieve only 77--82\\% while compact exception events reach ${\\geq}$99.33\\%.\nA regex exploiting format markers matches this ceiling, but marker removal collapses it to 28.49\\% while SFT retains 97.65\\%; cross-dataset transfer shows SFT acquires diagnostic patterns beyond format matching.\nGradient masking attributes ${\\leq}$4\\% of the 18pp degradation gap to gradient dilution, suggesting that diagnostic signal density at inference is the dominant factor, though forward-pass attention during masked training is an unquantified residual.\nAn empirical two-tier signal hierarchy holds across families, yielding a practical strategy: extract exception events first; when unavailable, tail-truncated traces provide a viable fallback.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{introduction}\n\\input{method}\n\\input{experiments}\n\\input{discussion}\n\\input{related_work}\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur study has several limitations.\nFirst, all experiments use HumanEval and MBPP-sanitized (591 problems, simple assert-based tests on short Python functions); generalization to human-written code, other languages, non-assert test frameworks (e.g., parametric tests, integration tests, flaky tests), and multi-file projects is unverified.\nSecond, all four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; models at 30B+ parameters may have sufficient capacity to overcome diagnostic signal dilution, and general-purpose LLMs may behave differently.\nThird, multi-seed experiments use only 3 seeds (the minimum for variance estimation); larger seed pools would strengthen robustness claims.\nFourth, \\texttt{full\\_trace} was not evaluated on DebugBench due to the supplementary nature of that analysis; whether complete traces degrade on human-written buggy code remains untested.\nFifth, the gradient masking ablation (\\S\\ref{sec:density}) uses a single model (Qwen-7B, 3 seeds); the complementary label-only analysis is limited to the Qwen family and does not reach significance under clustered bootstrap.\nSixth, VOTA's longer sequences are vulnerable to tokenizer-driven truncation: 1 of 12 model-seed combinations suffered catastrophic failure (DeepSeek seed 42: 84.45\\%), indicating that VOTA robustness depends on tokenizer efficiency.\nSeventh, several baselines (\\texttt{no\\_trace} for 2/4 models, \\texttt{full\\_trace} for 2/4 models) rely on single-seed results; their variance estimates are derived from Wilson CIs rather than empirical seed distributions.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work uses publicly available benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized, DebugBench) and does not involve human subjects, crowdsourced annotations, or personally identifiable information.\nAll models evaluated are open-source and publicly accessible.\nTraining uses parameter-efficient LoRA fine-tuning on enterprise-grade GPUs, with each experiment using a single GPU.\nTotal compute is approximately 320 GPU-hours across all conditions, models, seeds, and iterative refinements (including approximately 170 hours for successful experiments; individual runs range from 0.5h for small models to 2.2h for CodeLlama-13B, with the total reflecting 4 models $\\times$ multiple conditions $\\times$ multiple seeds $\\times$ iterative development).\nWe do not foresee direct negative societal impacts from this work; verdict prediction is a developer-facing tool for automated testing.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",
        "numLines": 60,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 60
      }
```

```json
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{A descriptive two-pathway framework.}\nThe results suggest a descriptive two-pathway framework (not a causal model) for why complete traces degrade SFT learning.\n\n\\paragraph{Explicit pathway: diagnostic tokens.}\nThe diagnostic token ratio $d$ (\\S2.1) correlates with accuracy at $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$, df=3; treat as descriptive) across five conditions, and all four models achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% under \\texttt{exception\\_only} with zero variance on Qwen ($\\sigma{=}$0.00, 3 seeds).\nAlthough 55.5\\% of failures are silent logic errors, \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 100\\% fail recall because test assertions generate \\texttt{AssertionError} events.\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}, a strict superset, slightly underperforms \\texttt{exception\\_only} (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), indicating that additional content introduces noise.\n\n\\paragraph{Implicit pathway: terminal execution state.}\nTail-truncated traces achieve 93--97\\% accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}) despite $d {\\approx} 0$.\nTail truncation at ${\\geq}$512 tokens captures \\texttt{[Line~X]} variable values at final execution steps, which indirectly encode program outcome; below 384 tokens, the globals module dump consumes the entire budget, yielding ${\\sim}$73\\% ($\\approx$ \\texttt{no\\_trace}).\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic signal dilution in complete traces.}\nBoth pathways' signals exist in full traces but are buried under thousands of intermediate tokens.\nControlled gradient masking supports this: mean $-$0.61pp under 99.8\\% mask (3 seeds) limits gradient dilution to ${\\leq}$4\\% of the gap (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics} shows: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps (std\\,=\\,0 by step 200), while \\texttt{full\\_trace} oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence (final std\\,=\\,0.014); eval accuracy CV differs 9.4$\\times$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}\n\\caption{Training dynamics: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges to 99.83\\% by step 150; \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence.}\n\\label{fig:training_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Why SFT over regex?}\nSFT \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%) exceeds the best regex (B5, 99.33\\%) by 0.50pp on caught-exception boundary cases (McNemar $p{=}.014$), provides calibrated probabilities (ECE\\,=\\,0.0027; \\S\\ref{sec:calibration}), and retains 97.65\\% under marker removal vs.\\ B5's 28.49\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\nBidirectional cross-dataset transfer (99.4--100\\%) with near-majority \\texttt{no\\_trace} performance shows the learned patterns are language-level, not dataset-specific.\n\n\\paragraph{The marginal value of complete traces.}\nSource code alone achieves 73--76\\% (\\texttt{no\\_trace}), so traces add at most ${\\sim}$27pp; complete traces capture only 4--9pp of this headroom while exception events capture 24--27pp.\nThat more information degrades performance aligns with feature selection theory \\citep{guyon2003introduction}, the Information Bottleneck \\citep{tishby2000information}, and LIMA \\citep{zhou2023lima}.\n\n\\paragraph{Design implications.}\nThe two-tier hierarchy suggests a practical fallback: extract exception events first; resort to tail-truncated state otherwise.\nTier~2 benefit is model-dependent (Qwen +10.9pp; CodeLlama +0.9pp), so trace design should match deployment model capacity.\n\n",
        "numLines": 40,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 40
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/discussion.tex"
```

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "2fc89224-917d-4fff-a6bf-3322eb0d3fba",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/method.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/method.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Method}\n\n\\subsection{Task Definition and Diagnostic Signal Metrics}\n\nWe study \\emph{verdict prediction}: given a Python function, a test input, and an optional execution trace, predict whether the test passes or fails.\nThe model produces a single token (\\texttt{pass} or \\texttt{fail}), making this a binary classification task.\nUnlike code generation \\citep{chen2025tracepile} or output prediction \\citep{gu2024cruxeval}, verdict prediction isolates the model's ability to detect faults from its ability to simulate execution, providing cleaner ablation contrasts than generation tasks.\n\nTo quantify signal quality without circular reference to model accuracy, we define the \\emph{diagnostic token ratio} $d = n_{\\text{diag}} / n_{\\text{total}}$, where $n_{\\text{diag}}$ counts tokens matching exception or assertion patterns.\nThis model-free metric correlates with accuracy at Pearson $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$, $N{=}5$, df=3; treat as descriptive), but captures only the \\emph{explicit} diagnostic pathway; tail-truncated conditions achieve 93--97\\% with $d {\\approx} 0$, indicating a second, implicit signal channel operating through terminal execution state (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\n\n\\subsection{Dataset}\n\\label{sec:dataset}\n\nWe sampled 10 candidate solutions per problem from Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct (temperature 0.8, top\\_p 0.95) for 591 problems from HumanEval \\citep{chen2021evaluating} (164) and MBPP-sanitized \\citep{austin2021program} (427), executed each against canonical tests with \\texttt{sys.settrace} to obtain line-level traces and pass/fail verdicts, yielding 5,910 pairs (67\\% pass, 33\\% fail).\nThe dataset is split 80/20 by problem ID into 4,720 training and 1,190 test samples (majority baseline: 71.51\\%).\nThe \\texttt{exception\\_only} format collapses passing solutions into identical rows (39.45\\% train, 48.99\\% test duplicates); these duplicates arise from multiple candidate solutions per problem, with the train--test split enforced at the problem level (472 train / 119 test problems, zero crossover; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dedup_stats}).\n\n\\subsection{Trace Representations}\n\nWe define seven conditions that vary the trace information available (Figure~\\ref{fig:trace_overview}):\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\texttt{no\\_trace}: Source code and test input only.\n\\item \\texttt{full\\_trace}: Complete line-by-line trace with variable states.\n\\item \\texttt{VOTA} (Verdict-Optimized Trace Abstraction): Exception events + assertion-point comparisons + loop compression (Appendix~\\ref{sec:vota_algorithm}).\n\\item \\texttt{exception\\_only}: Only exception/error events.\n\\item \\texttt{ae\\_only}: Only assertion-point actual/expected pairs.\n\\item \\texttt{loop\\_only}: Only loop compression summaries.\n\\item \\texttt{VOTA\\_no\\_ae}: VOTA without assertion-point information.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig1_trace_overview}\n\\caption{Overview of the verdict prediction pipeline. Source code and a test input are executed to produce a full raw trace. Four conditions vary the trace information: \\texttt{no\\_trace} (75.13\\%), \\texttt{full\\_trace} (81.44\\%), VOTA (99.05\\%), and \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%). Accuracies for Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$); multi-seed means where available (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}). Appendix~\\ref{sec:vota_examples} provides input/output examples.}\n\\label{fig:trace_overview}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Models and Training}\n\nWe evaluate four models spanning three architecture families: \\textbf{Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B/1.5B-Instruct} \\citep{qwen2024coder}, \\textbf{CodeLlama-13B-Instruct} \\citep{roziere2024code}, and \\textbf{DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct} \\citep{deepseek2024coder}, covering a 1.5B--13B parameter range (8.7$\\times$).\nAll conditions use LoRA \\citep{hu2022lora} (rank=16, $\\alpha$=32, dropout=0.05) on all linear layers, 3 epochs, effective batch size 32, LR $2 \\times 10^{-4}$ with linear warmup/decay, max sequence length 2{,}048 tokens.\nSequences exceeding this limit are truncated while preserving the verdict label.\nThe primary seed is 42; seeds 123 and 456 test \\texttt{exception\\_only} robustness (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_details}).\n\n\\subsection{Loss Ablation}\n\nTo probe the contribution of gradient dilution, we introduce \\texttt{full\\_trace\\_label\\_only}: identical full-trace input but loss computed only on the verdict token (trace positions masked).\nTRL's SFTTrainer computes mean cross-entropy over label tokens, so label-only loss does not amplify the effective learning rate.\nHowever, this ablation simultaneously changes three variables: (1)~which tokens receive gradient updates, (2)~the implicit regularization from predicting trace tokens, and (3)~the training signal distribution.\nThe resulting improvement over standard \\texttt{full\\_trace} provides a confounded upper bound on gradient dilution's contribution.\nA cleaner test (masking 99.8\\% of gradient updates during \\texttt{exception\\_only} training) matches the diagnostic token ratio of full traces while preserving compact input, isolating gradient dilution from content quality (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\n",
        "numLines": 53,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 53
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/method.tex"
  },
  "type": "attachment",
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01KCz2c3YBFzJWSYyNd7j5c7`</summary>

```text
125	To quantify the marker's contribution, we retrain with all format markers removed (\texttt{nomarker} condition).
126	
127	Qwen-7B 3-seed mean: 97.65\% $\pm$ 0.96 (seeds 42/123/456: 96.55/98.32/98.07\%), a drop of 2.18pp from \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\%).
128	Qwen-1.5B: 97.82\%, drop of 2.01pp.
129	The marker contribution (${\sim}$2pp) accounts for only ${\sim}$9\% of the total exception lift over \texttt{no\_trace} (24.70pp on 7B).
130	Regex baselines confirm this decomposition: B5 (\texttt{[No exception data]}$\to$pass, 99.33\% with markers) collapses to 28.49\% without markers, showing it is entirely format-dependent.
131	The strongest semantic regex B4 (exception class matching, 93.61\%) is marker-invariant; SFT \texttt{nomarker} (97.65\%) exceeds it by 4.04pp, isolating the learned semantic contribution beyond pattern matching.
132	
133	\paragraph{Cross-dataset transfer.}
134	Bidirectional evaluation demonstrates generalization: HumanEval${\to}$MBPP \texttt{exception\_only} achieves 99.41\% ($N{=}4{,}270$) and MBPP${\to}$HumanEval achieves 100.0\% ($N{=}1{,}640$), while \texttt{no\_trace} drops to near-majority baseline in both directions (70.56\% vs.\ 69.02\% majority; 67.99\% vs.\ 61.65\%).
135	The near-zero transfer loss for exception signals, across independently authored problem sets with no problem overlap, shows SFT learns language-level diagnostic patterns, not dataset-specific artifacts.
136	Under marker removal, cross-dataset accuracy drops to 95.5\% ($-$3.9pp), indicating that the majority of cross-dataset transfer relies on learned diagnostic patterns rather than format markers.
137	
138	\subsection{Decomposing the Full-Trace Degradation}
139	\label{sec:density}
```

### Tool result

_source line 8769 · 2026-05-22T18:29:59.059Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01AK3VtTdxC2u7Zg7v2uhK3N`</summary>

```text
190	
191	A cross-model budget sweep across four models and three architecture families reveals a sharp performance jump localized between 384 and 512 tail tokens.
192	At tail-384, all models remain near baseline (73.6--80.7\%, only +1.0--4.8pp over tail-256); the steep jump occurs at 384$\to$512 (+13.2--22.0pp), crossing from near-baseline to near-ceiling (${\sim}$94--98\%).
193	This localizes the critical threshold to ${\sim}$400--500 tokens, crossing the globals module dump threshold (${\sim}$384 tokens) and exposing structured execution markers (Table~\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}).
194	CodeLlama tail conditions use label-only loss (\S\ref{sec:density}); cross-model tail comparisons should account for this training difference.
195	Beyond 512 tokens, accuracy converges further: multi-seed validation across all 12 model$\times$seed combinations confirms all tail-512 accuracies fall within a 4.63pp band [93.61\%, 98.24\%] (Table~\ref{tab:tail512_seeds}); at tail-1024, three models reach 96.5--98.2\%, compressing the 6.3pp cross-model spread observed at tail-256.
196	\texttt{tail\_2048} captures 95--98\% of \texttt{exception\_only}'s accuracy across all completed configurations.
197	The residual 2--5pp gap confirms that semantic extraction (identifying exception type and message) still outperforms positional extraction (retaining a fixed window containing non-diagnostic lines).
198	
199	\paragraph{Calibration.}
200	\label{sec:calibration}
201	Calibration mirrors the tier hierarchy: \texttt{exception\_only} achieves ECE = 0.0027 vs.\ 0.171 for \texttt{no\_trace}, a 63$\times$ gap (Figure~\ref{fig:calibration}).
202	This calibration advantage generalizes across architectures and scales: all four models (1.5B--13B) achieve ECE ${<}$ 0.005 under \texttt{exception\_only} (Table~\ref{tab:calibration_model_level} in Appendix), indicating well-calibrated predictions rather than overfit confidence.
203	
204	\paragraph{Error analysis.}
205	\texttt{exception\_only}'s 2 errors have zero overlap with all other conditions' error sets (Jaccard = 0.000; Appendix~\ref{sec:error_overlap}); per-type breakdown confirms all standard exception types achieve 100\% accuracy, with only \texttt{GeneratorExit} (a benign \texttt{BaseException} subclass) causing failures (Appendix~\ref{sec:exception_type}).
206	
207	\paragraph{Length control.}
208	Padding \texttt{exception\_only} to 2{,}048 tokens, even in a semantically loaded position (after the verdict region), yields identical accuracy (99.83\%), indicating the 18--23pp gap arises from spurious patterns in verbose trace content, not sequence length.
209	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_013QRy7xnxQsR5yFGduxDk7z`</summary>

```text
147:In the pass case, the absence of an exception event combined with a matching assertion point confirms correct execution.
173:No exception is raised, and the assertion point confirms that the actual output matches the expected value.
257:Re-evaluation of the seed 42 checkpoint with the standard max\_length=2048 yields 16\% accuracy, confirming complete model collapse rather than marginal degradation.
265:Per-class analysis (Table~\ref{tab:per_class}) confirms that seed 42 achieves 100\% fail precision but only 45.43\% fail recall, systematically defaulting to \texttt{pass}.
326:This confirms that the model has learned a clean decision boundary: any true \texttt{Exception} subclass in the trace implies failure.
353:All three subcomponents independently achieve ${\geq}$99.50\% with 100\% fail recall (Table~\ref{tab:exc_subcomponent}), confirming that no single subcomponent is necessary for Tier~1 performance.
437:All four trace budget conditions significantly exceed \texttt{no\_trace} except 1024 tokens ($p{=}.078$), confirming that raw trace content provides some signal but unreliably so.
456:The fact that \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\%) still outperforms \texttt{tail\_2048} (97.31\%) confirms that semantic extraction (identifying the exception type and message) is superior to positional extraction (keeping a fixed window), because the model need not filter out non-diagnostic lines that happen to fall within the window.
506:Multi-seed validation confirms robustness: across all 12 model$\times$seed combinations at tail-512 (Table~\ref{tab:tail512_seeds}), all accuracies fall within [93.61\%, 98.24\%], a 4.63pp band that compresses the signal-poor tail-256 spread (6.3pp) by 27\%.
538:In the signal-rich regime (tail-512+), this reverses: Qwen-1.5B matches or exceeds Qwen-7B (95.6\% vs.\ 93.6\% at tail-512; 97.9\% vs.\ 97.3\% at tail-2048), confirming that sufficient trace signal eliminates the capacity advantage.
545:Table~\ref{tab:head_vs_tail} confirms the truncation direction effect: among the three models with complete data, tail-2048 outperforms head-2048 by 15.5--22.6pp.
580:All four models achieve ECE ${<}$ 0.007 and Brier ${<}$ 0.007, confirming that calibration quality generalizes across architectures and scales.
619:This analysis confirms that \texttt{exception\_only} operates on an independent signal dimension: its errors are representation ambiguities (iterator protocol artifacts) rather than information deficits, explaining why combining it with other signals cannot eliminate these specific errors.
624:\caption{Jaccard similarity between error sets of different conditions (Qwen-7B, seed=42, $N{=}1{,}190$). \texttt{exception\_only} (exc\_only) has zero overlap with all other conditions, confirming it operates on an independent signal dimension.}
654:The \texttt{exception\_only} condition achieves perfect accuracy, confirming that the exception-based representation generalizes to a different fault distribution without degradation.
686:Seeds 123/456, which avoid the truncation-sensitive initialization basin, achieve 99.16\%/99.33\%, confirming that the deficit is seed-specific rather than a fundamental model limitation.
754:B5 (99.33\%) achieves near-SFT accuracy but relies on the deterministic presence of a format marker in pass-class traces, which is an artifact of the trace preprocessing pipeline rather than a generalizable signal; McNemar test vs.\ SFT yields $p{=}.041$, confirming a statistically significant difference.
812:\caption{Model calibration on \texttt{exception\_only} (seed 42). All four models achieve ECE $<$ 0.005, confirming that near-perfect accuracy reflects genuine confidence rather than overfit predictions.}
```

```text
253	\section{Tokenizer and Truncation Analysis}
254	\label{sec:tokenizer_analysis}
255	
256	DeepSeek VOTA seed 42 (84.45\%) is a training failure driven by tokenizer-induced truncation.
257	Re-evaluation of the seed 42 checkpoint with the standard max\_length=2048 yields 16\% accuracy, confirming complete model collapse rather than marginal degradation.
258	The previously reported 84.45\% was obtained with max\_length=16384, which partially bypasses tokenizer truncation.
259	Seeds 123 and 456, evaluated under identical conditions, achieve 99.16\% and 99.33\% respectively.
260	The DeepSeek tokenizer averages 2{,}626 tokens per VOTA sample versus 1{,}929 for Qwen, causing 91.7\% of DeepSeek VOTA training samples to exceed the 2{,}048-token context window.
261	The training framework preserves the verdict label but truncates trace content, so the model trains with incomplete diagnostic information for the majority of samples.
262	Seeds 123 and 456 (99.16\% and 99.33\%) demonstrate that DeepSeek can learn VOTA when LoRA initialization avoids the truncation-sensitive basin; the 2-seed mean (99.25\%$\pm$0.12) matches the cross-model VOTA range (98.74--99.54\%).
263	
264	A post-hoc group analysis on seed 42 provides directional evidence: partitioning test samples by whether their training-set counterparts exceeded the context window, intact samples ($N{=}99$) achieve 100\% accuracy while truncated samples ($N{=}1{,}091$) achieve 83.04\% (95\% CI [80.75\%, 85.24\%]; Fisher exact $p{<}.001$), with all 185 errors concentrated in the fail class (fail recall = 31.23\%).
265	Per-class analysis (Table~\ref{tab:per_class}) confirms that seed 42 achieves 100\% fail precision but only 45.43\% fail recall, systematically defaulting to \texttt{pass}.
266	
267	Seed robustness is consistent across models: Qwen \texttt{exception\_only} produces identical 99.83\% across all three seeds ($\sigma{=}0$), and DeepSeek achieves 99.39\%$\pm$0.10 (seeds 42/456: 99.33\%, seed 123: 99.50\%).
268	Because \texttt{exception\_only} test inputs contain 48.99\% duplicates (607 unique of 1,190; \S\ref{sec:dataset}), CIs assume $n{=}1{,}190$ independent trials; under deduplication ($n{=}607$), Qwen's accuracy is 99.67\% (Wilson CI [98.81, 99.91]).
```

```text
433	This non-monotonicity is specific to head-truncation: the 1024-token prefix retains enough irrelevant content to mislead the model while discarding diagnostic events that appear near the trace terminus.
434	Under tail-truncation, accuracy increases monotonically for Qwen-7B and Qwen-1.5B (Table~\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}); only DeepSeek-6.7B shows a mild $-$1.5pp decrease at tail-1024$\to$tail-2048, attributable to architecture-specific long-context sensitivity rather than a systematic mid-length pathology.
435	
436	\paragraph{Comparison to baselines.}
437	All four trace budget conditions significantly exceed \texttt{no\_trace} except 1024 tokens ($p{=}.078$), confirming that raw trace content provides some signal but unreliably so.
438	The gap to \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\%) is massive for every budget condition (18--23pp, all $p{<}.001$), underscoring that even unlimited raw traces cannot substitute for targeted signal extraction.
439	The best full-trace condition (no\_limit, 81.76\%) still lags \texttt{exception\_only} by 18.07pp (95\% CI [$-$20.34, $-$15.88]).
440	
```

```text
502	The steep jump occurs at 384$\to$512: +13.7pp (Qwen-7B), +13.2pp (DeepSeek), +15.5pp (CodeLlama), +22.0pp (Qwen-1.5B), crossing from near-baseline to near-ceiling.
503	This localizes the critical threshold to ${\sim}$400--500 tokens: below ${\sim}$384 tokens, the entire token budget is consumed by globals module dumps (pure noise); above ${\sim}$512, at least one \texttt{[Line~X]} execution event marker becomes visible, regardless of model capacity or architecture.
504	
505	\emph{Convergence at tail-512+}: once the sharp performance jump is crossed, accuracy converges across all models into a 93.6--98.2\% band at tail-512 (seed 42).
506	Multi-seed validation confirms robustness: across all 12 model$\times$seed combinations at tail-512 (Table~\ref{tab:tail512_seeds}), all accuracies fall within [93.61\%, 98.24\%], a 4.63pp band that compresses the signal-poor tail-256 spread (6.3pp) by 27\%.
507	CodeLlama-13B shows perfect per-model convergence at tail-512: all three seeds achieve 98.24\% ($\sigma{=}$0), consistent with the 13B scale and label-only loss providing a stronger optimization signal in the signal-rich regime (Table~\ref{tab:tail512_seeds}).
508	However, CodeLlama's higher tail-512 accuracy may partly reflect label-only loss rather than model capacity; direct comparison with other models (which use full-sequence loss) is confounded by this training difference.
509
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_013jLLxZzrHpfun8gHxp4s7G`</summary>

```text
452	The critical jump occurs between 256 and 512 tail tokens (+16.13pp), localizing the signal window to approximately 256--512 tokens from the trace terminus.
453	This is consistent with the trace format: the globals module dump consumes the first ${\sim}$384 tail tokens (pure noise), so structured execution markers (\texttt{[Line~X]} variable values) only become visible when the budget exceeds this threshold.
454	
455	The 18.07pp gap from \texttt{full\_trace} to \texttt{exception\_only} decomposes as: 86\% positional concentration (recovered by tail-truncation at 2048 tokens) and 14\% residual content noise (the 2.52pp gap from \texttt{tail\_2048} to \texttt{exception\_only} that persists regardless of truncation direction).
456	The fact that \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\%) still outperforms \texttt{tail\_2048} (97.31\%) confirms that semantic extraction (identifying the exception type and message) is superior to positional extraction (keeping a fixed window), because the model need not filter out non-diagnostic lines that happen to fall within the window.
457	
458	\paragraph{Cross-model budget curve.}
459	Table~\ref{tab:tail_cross_model} extends the tail-truncation analysis to a full budget sweep across four models spanning three architecture families and an 8.7$\times$ parameter range.
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01JwVPyLnF7Pneq9hEYJKWcX`</summary>

```text
534	\emph{Signal-poor convergence band}: head-2048 accuracies across all four models also converge into a narrow 75.3--81.8\% band (6.5pp; Table~\ref{tab:head_vs_tail}), comparable in width to the tail-256 band (72.6--78.9\%, 6.3pp).
535	When the diagnostic signal is absent (head-truncation discards trace terminus) or unreachable (tail-256 is too short), models of different scale and architecture collapse to similar performance, suggesting a shared floor determined by code comprehension rather than trace processing.
536	
537	\emph{Scale behavior inversion}: in the signal-poor regime (tail-256), smaller models perform worse (Qwen-1.5B 72.6\% vs.\ Qwen-7B 78.9\%, $-$6.3pp), mirroring the \texttt{no\_trace} scale pattern.
538	In the signal-rich regime (tail-512+), this reverses: Qwen-1.5B matches or exceeds Qwen-7B (95.6\% vs.\ 93.6\% at tail-512; 97.9\% vs.\ 97.3\% at tail-2048), confirming that sufficient trace signal eliminates the capacity advantage.
539	
540	\emph{Non-monotonicity at long sequences}: two models show accuracy decreases beyond tail-1024.
541	DeepSeek-6.7B drops $-$1.5pp from tail-1024 (96.5\%) to tail-2048 (95.0\%), consistent with its known long-context sensitivity (\S\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).
542	CodeLlama-13B exhibits a more severe collapse: tail-2048 under label-only loss drops to 41.8\% (vs.\ tail-1024 = 98.2\%), which we attribute to LoRA long-sequence instability under the label-only loss regime; this outlier is excluded from cross-model tail-2048 analysis.
543	Qwen-7B and Qwen-1.5B increase monotonically across all budgets.
544	
545	Table~\ref{tab:head_vs_tail} confirms the truncation direction effect: among the three models with complete data, tail-2048 outperforms head-2048 by 15.5--22.6pp.
546	The gap is largest for Qwen-1.5B (+22.6pp), indicating that smaller models benefit most from positional proximity to the diagnostic signal, as they lack the capacity to locate it within longer, noisier prefixes.
547	
548	%% ============================================================
549	\section{Calibration Analysis}
```

```text
615	
616	Among non-exception conditions, error sets exhibit hierarchical containment: \texttt{ae\_only} $\subset$ VOTA $\subset$ \texttt{loop\_only} $\subset$ \texttt{no\_trace}, with pairwise Jaccard indices increasing with signal overlap.
617	\texttt{loop\_only} and \texttt{full\_trace} share the highest overlap (Jaccard ${\sim}$0.65), reflecting their shared reliance on behavioral patterns rather than explicit fault indicators.
618	
619	This analysis confirms that \texttt{exception\_only} operates on an independent signal dimension: its errors are representation ambiguities (iterator protocol artifacts) rather than information deficits, explaining why combining it with other signals cannot eliminate these specific errors.
```

```text
650	\end{tabular}
651	\end{table}
652	
653	Table~\ref{tab:debugbench} shows that the component ranking from the main experiments is preserved: \texttt{exception\_only} (100.00\%) $\geq$ VOTA (99.77\%) $>$ \texttt{no\_trace} (96.71\%).
654	The \texttt{exception\_only} condition achieves perfect accuracy, confirming that the exception-based representation generalizes to a different fault distribution without degradation.
655	
656	The absolute margins are much narrower than on the main dataset: the \texttt{no\_trace} baseline already achieves 96.71\% (compared to 75.13\% on HumanEval+MBPP), leaving only 3.29pp of headroom for trace-based improvement.
657	This ceiling effect reflects a fundamental difference in fault characteristics: DebugBench bugs are synthetically injected single-point mutations, which produce code that is structurally dissimilar from the original correct solution; a model can often identify the fault from source code alone.
658	In contrast, the HumanEval+MBPP solutions are LLM-generated, producing natural errors (logic mistakes, edge-case failures) where the faulty and correct solutions may be structurally similar, making code-only verdict prediction substantially harder.
659	
```

```text
682	Early stopping may yield marginal gains, but the epoch effect is small (${\leq}$0.5pp).
683	The practical recommendation: for deployment, use task-specific metrics (accuracy, fail recall) for checkpoint and seed selection rather than training loss, and train multiple seeds to select the best.
684	
685	The contrast with VOTA is instructive: DeepSeek VOTA seed 42 accuracy monotonically increases from 78.15\% to 84.45\% across epochs, consistent with a performance limit from high trace truncation (91.7\% of samples; \S\ref{sec:tokenizer_analysis}).
686	Seeds 123/456, which avoid the truncation-sensitive initialization basin, achieve 99.16\%/99.33\%, confirming that the deficit is seed-specific rather than a fundamental model limitation.
687
```

_source line 8817 · 2026-05-22T18:30:44.108Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_011UGoTE8jsasihLMB2kFocm`</summary>

```text
750	
751	The strongest legitimate baseline (B4, 93.61\%) applies regex pattern-matching on Python exception class names (e.g., \texttt{ValueError}, \texttt{TypeError}).
752	Its errors concentrate on 8 boundary samples where a \texttt{ValueError} is raised inside a \texttt{try/except} block and caught; the program passes despite an exception occurring in the trace.
753	SFT \texttt{exception\_only} correctly classifies 6 of these 8 samples, indicating that the model learns the semantic distinction between caught (benign) and uncaught (fault-indicating) exceptions.
754	B5 (99.33\%) achieves near-SFT accuracy but relies on the deterministic presence of a format marker in pass-class traces, which is an artifact of the trace preprocessing pipeline rather than a generalizable signal; McNemar test vs.\ SFT yields $p{=}.041$, confirming a statistically significant difference.
755	
756	\paragraph{Cross-format comparison.}
757	Table~\ref{tab:regex_cross_format} compares SFT and the best applicable regex baseline for each trace format.
```
